        "resolution": "Plan Step 2 + cli.py path resolution unchanged."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-P9-022",
        "concern": "Plan Step 4 fixes one production subprocess site (executor runner.py:308-312) but defers legacy hype/run.py and other potential subprocess.run/Popen sites. A thorough audit of all subprocess invocations was not performed; AUTHOR_TEST may still leak via these paths if any of them is reached by a code-step argv during author-test.",
        "resolution": "Plan assumption 5 explicitly defers legacy hype/run.py."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-P9-023",
        "concern": "Plan updates _cmd_test to use orch-scoped fixture/golden layout but does NOT update _cmd_new (cli.py:265-271) which still scaffolds at the legacy pack-scoped layout (`<pack>/fixtures/<orch>/.keep`, `<pack>/golden/<orch>.events.jsonl`). After the plan ships, `author new sample.foo` creates files that `author test` cannot find. The two author verbs become inconsistent.",
        "resolution": "Revision 5 applies surgical fixes to all 13 open significant flags from the gate critique. (1) Code-step ordering fixed: gate_command runs first (applies TASK_* env), then subprocess.run, then record_dispatch_complete (resolves correctness-1). (2) Max-iterations guard added: after 5 consecutive same-cursor visits the test loop aborts with a clear message, preventing infinite spin on produces_check_failed \u2192 cursor_rewind cycles (resolves correctness-2, FLAG-P9-026). (3) Missing-golden UX: explicit 'no golden file at <path>; run with --regenerate to create it' check before diff, exit 2 (resolves correctness-3). (4) Fixture collision: shutil.copytree now targets <project>/inputs/ subdirectory, and the constraint on reserved filenames is documented in AUTHORING.md (resolves correctness-4). (5) Step 14 test setup fixed: compiled plan path corrected to builtin/build/hype.json matching compile.py:142 (resolves issue_hints-1). (6) _cmd_new layout cluster (6 flags: issue_hints-2, scope-1, scope-2, all_locations-1, all_locations-2, FLAG-P9-023): _cmd_new now scaffolds the new orch-scoped layout and test_author_cli.py assertions updated accordingly \u2014 no deferred inconsistency. (7) Stub input: inputs/input.txt committed in the smoke fixture to exercise fixture-copy (resolves issue_hints-3). (8) New tests added for max-iterations guard and missing-golden error. (9) Known limitations section expanded with all documented edges."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-P9-024",
        "concern": "Plan Step 12 specifies the test setup to place the compiled plan at `builtin/hype/build/hype.json` but compile_to_path writes to `<pack_root>/build/<name>.json` = `builtin/build/hype.json`. Plan Step 7b correctly resolves the path to `pack_root / 'build' / orch_id.json`. Step 12 is internally inconsistent with Step 7b and the actual compile output location.",
        "resolution": "Plan Step 12 path string + compile.py:142 + plan Step 7b."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-P9-025",
        "concern": "Plan Step 7i runs subprocess.run BEFORE gate_command for code steps. apply_task_run_env (which sets TASK_STEP_ID/TASK_RUN_ID/TASK_PROJECT/TASK_ITEM_ID/TASK_ITERATION) runs INSIDE gate_command. So when child_subprocess_env() snapshots os.environ for the subprocess, those task vars are absent. For the smoke argv (relative-path file write) this is harmless, but any future fixture whose code argv reads task env vars (e.g. via `artagents executors run`) would break. Conventional production order (gate_command \u2192 subprocess \u2192 record_dispatch_complete) sets env first.",
        "resolution": "Plan Step 7i subprocess.run before gate_command + gate.py:817/832/848 apply_task_run_env."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-P9-026",
        "concern": "Plan Step 7i loop has no termination guard against produces_check_failed \u2192 cursor_rewind cycles. Under auto-approval, attested steps always succeed, but a buggy code-step argv can cause produces_check_failed which the gate handles by NOT advancing the cursor. peek_current_step returns the same CodeStep on every iteration, subprocess runs again, fails again, loops forever. The test loop should add a max-iteration guard (e.g. abort after the same cursor is seen N times) to fail fast.",
        "resolution": "Revision 5 applies surgical fixes to all 13 open significant flags from the gate critique. (1) Code-step ordering fixed: gate_command runs first (applies TASK_* env), then subprocess.run, then record_dispatch_complete (resolves correctness-1). (2) Max-iterations guard added: after 5 consecutive same-cursor visits the test loop aborts with a clear message, preventing infinite spin on produces_check_failed \u2192 cursor_rewind cycles (resolves correctness-2, FLAG-P9-026). (3) Missing-golden UX: explicit 'no golden file at <path>; run with --regenerate to create it' check before diff, exit 2 (resolves correctness-3). (4) Fixture collision: shutil.copytree now targets <project>/inputs/ subdirectory, and the constraint on reserved filenames is documented in AUTHORING.md (resolves correctness-4). (5) Step 14 test setup fixed: compiled plan path corrected to builtin/build/hype.json matching compile.py:142 (resolves issue_hints-1). (6) _cmd_new layout cluster (6 flags: issue_hints-2, scope-1, scope-2, all_locations-1, all_locations-2, FLAG-P9-023): _cmd_new now scaffolds the new orch-scoped layout and test_author_cli.py assertions updated accordingly \u2014 no deferred inconsistency. (7) Stub input: inputs/input.txt committed in the smoke fixture to exercise fixture-copy (resolves issue_hints-3). (8) New tests added for max-iterations guard and missing-golden error. (9) Known limitations section expanded with all documented edges."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-P9-027",
        "concern": "Plan Step 7k loads the golden file via difflib.unified_diff without checking existence. If the golden is missing AND --regenerate is not set, `Path.read_text()` raises FileNotFoundError instead of a clean error message instructing the user to run with --regenerate. Minor UX gap.",
        "resolution": "Plan Step 7k."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-P9-028",
        "concern": "Plan Step 7e copies fixture contents into `projects_root / slug` (the project_dir). cmd_start subsequently writes `active_run.json`, `plan.json`, and creates `runs/<run_id>/`. If a fixture happens to ship reserved filenames (`active_run.json`, `plan.json`, `runs/`), they'd collide with cmd_start's writes (or worse, be silently overwritten). Plan should either copy into a subdirectory like `projects_root / slug / 'inputs/'` or document the reserved-filename constraint.",
        "resolution": "Plan Step 7e + lifecycle.py:176-205 cmd_start writes."
      }

        Robustness level:
        standard

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```
